# 注意事項：

1.  $^{14}\mathrm{C}$  年代值はBPの单位で、西曆1950年か過去へ遜た年代值て示てる。

2.  $^{14}\mathrm{C}$  的半减期とて、國際的用いにてLibbyの半減期5，568年を用て  $^{14}\mathrm{C}$  年代值を算出た

3.  $^{14}\mathrm{C}$  年代值の誤差はone sigma（±1σ；1標準偏差）を示た。こは、同比条件で測定を100回操り返ししぃと、測定結果が誤差範圍内に入の割合が68回earing事を意味する。誤差を表示の2倍（±2σ；2標準偏差）にと、誤差範圍に入の割合は95回にな。

4.  $\delta^{13}\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{PDB}}$  用以炭素同位体分别的补正行。

$^{14}\mathrm{C}$  年代值か層年代の較正は、樹木年輪にての濃度測定か得た較正テ一を用る。ごは、IntCal09較正テ一（Reimer et al.2009，Radiocarbon,51(4)，p.1111-1151）と較正ロガムOxCal 4.1（Bronk Ramsey 2001；2009，Radiocarbon,43（2A）355-363;51（1）337-360）用いて較正を行った。

\*磨年代は，14C年代值の誤差範圍と14C年代值－磨年代較正曲線上の関係か，真的年代が入る可能性が高い磨年代範围て示。可能性の範圍は複数あ員合には，その等にて可能性の確率が示を。真の年代が、表示たすての範圍のとかに入る確率は1標準偏差の场合は68.2%（1σ），2標準偏差の场合は95.4%（2σ）て見。

# 后记

很多遗址是在不经意间发现的，而且其收获往往会出乎人们意料。卞家山试掘点当时就是一片低洼的水田，挖去耕作层就是所谓的小粉土了。按之前的经验，这种纯净的粉性黄土或青灰土基本可算作生土。但是偏偏露了几个木桩，职业的敏感让我们继续扩土寻找，然后就有了遗址边缘的坡地，有了水滨的淤积层，有了成排的木桩。至此已无法收手，随着揭露范围的展推，墩体部位的遗存渐次显露真容。于是我们知道，朝南的水滨有一个栈桥连着水埠的木构码头，码头倚靠着草裹泥为基础的人工台地；台地内侧有两条先后沿用的宽大灰沟，灰沟的一端与南面的水域贯通，另一端却断头于东面的居址；而灰沟的北侧，竟是一片延续很长时间的墓地。

经过三年多有间断的考古挖掘，卞家山西部的这一段被我们拦腰剖开，获得的居然是这样一幅良渚时期平凡而温馨的生活画卷。这让我们兴奋，也让我们备感清新。在此之前，我们关注的大多是显贵们拥有的精美玉器、显贵们独享的恢弘祭坛、显贵们生活和葬身的高大台地，而我们所见的平民遗存大多支离破碎、乏善可陈。因此，当下家山这样一幅兼具墓地、生活遗迹和水岸码头，充满水乡气息的世俗画卷跃入眼帘时，怎能不让人怦然心动呢？

如今，我们把这样一幅画卷转换成专业的图文，希望能引起业内人士的共鸣。这里面有很多我们可以思考的地方，比如：墓地与居住及活动区为何挨得那么近，墓地内何以有近半数的墓向朝北，边缘楔有木桩和竹篱笆的大型灰沟到底作何用，码头桩基之上如何架设踏板，桩基边上成排的竹篱笆与码头是否有直接关系，有两对穿孔的巨型木墩真的用来拴船吗，陶质房屋模型的完整形态会是怎样，四足方形盘的底部何故有个漏孔，人头骨瓢形容器是否为胜利者炫耀的物件，器物中缘何只见漆觚而不见陶觚，良渚晚期为什么也会出现粗泥陶，同形的刻划符号与戳点符号是否具有相同的含义。诸如此类，虽然我们无法给出确切答案，相信不同方向的研究者都能从中获取有用的资料和信息，进而迸发出某些灵感。

发掘当中也留下了一些遗憾和值得反思的地方，例如：发掘工作非连续，防护工作不够到位，致使探方壁和部分遗迹面遭到日晒雨淋的重度损毁，影响了部分遗迹的完整性和局部地层的对接；挖灰沟时面积过小，一些沟内堆积先按地层划分，大面积揭露时又调整为灰沟，致使部分遗物的归属出现偏差；台Ⅲ的营建非常考究，既有大片的草裹泥堆叠，又有团块土整齐的覆压，这样精巧的土台堆筑过程却未作仔细追踪与还原；漆器是难得保存下来的有机质文物，既珍贵又易氧化干裂，但因认识不足，出土时没有做完备的资料，漆木器脱水处理也不够慎重。如此等等，希望这些教训能让同仁们引以为戒。

令人欣慰的是，卡家山遗址还有很大一部分保存在民居下，只要村民们没有过深地扰动遗址本体，将来还有机会继续揭示这幅精彩生动的水乡画卷。就像反山墓地还有过半的面积存留于土墩内，它带

给我们的是无尽的遐想和迷人的诱惑。

卞家山发掘也是团队的成果，离不开每个成员的敬业奉献。队员中大多数为陕西籍技工，2004年有刘福刚、郑卓华两位，2005年有祁自立、孟小玲、张淑云、侯虎勤四位。刘福刚在炎热的夏季发掘阶段赤膊上阵，皮肤被晒得黑。祁自立是一名干将，对野外迹象有较强的分析处理能力。孟小玲的技能较全面，发掘、修复、整理、拓片都拿得起。本土技工葛建良参加卞家山遗址发掘的时间最长，先后参与了2003年和2005年两个年度的发掘，是个技术娴熟、办事勤快、善于与民工相处的好技工。2003年的发掘还有技工郭黎辉和陈武，试掘时陈欢乐也工作了一段时间。2005年发掘时，良渚文化博物馆新分入的大学生孙海波参加了实习，如今已成为良渚遗址管理所的所长。发掘过程中，郑云飞博士和后来进入本所的陈旭高先后多次到工地采集土样和淘洗植物种子。揭露木构码头和清理大型灰沟时，本所专业摄影师李永嘉也曾应邀到现场拍照和摄像，为我们留下了一些珍贵的影像资料。

卞家山发掘同样得到了相关领域专家的大力支持。他们是：玉石器鉴定——浙江大学地球科学系董传万教授，贝类遗存鉴定——日本福井县立恐龙博物馆野田芳和主任研究员、浙江省自然博物馆副馆长金幸生研究员，动物骨骼鉴定——北京大学研究生张颖，木材及植物遗存鉴定——本所科技室主任郑云飞博士、中国科学院南京地质古生物研究所黄翡研究员，有机质文物的保护处理——杭州市化工研究所的周文林、吴锦清工程师。

此外，卞家山遗址有缘纳入中日合作项目“长江下游地区新石器文化植物考古学研究”课题，在日方负责人中村镇一教授的联络下，部分日本学者参与了卞家山遗址动植物遗存的分析与检测，他们是：东北大学铃木三男教授（木材），奈良教育大学金原正明教授（硅藻、孢粉），奈良文化财研究所松井章教授（动物骨骼），宫崎大学羽田津教授（植物硅酸体）。金泽大学的中村镇一教授则带领学生参与了卞家山部分漆木器文物的绘图工作。

卞家山遗址还被纳入“第二期文明探源工程”，约40个测年样本送交北京大学碳十四标本实验室检测，最终由秦岭博士完成年代学分析报告。

本报告的线图描摹和多数器物绘图由张蕊侠完成，部分底图由葛建良绘制。陶器纹饰及符号的拓片主要由孟小玲完成，良渚文化博物馆（现为良渚博物院）的吴庆胜也主动要求为部分精细的陶器纹饰进行拓片。出土器物的图片由本所专职摄影师李永嘉拍摄。部分戳刻符号采用了张炳火主编、良渚博物院编著的《良渚文化刻画符号》的图片素材。北京大学的秦岭博士为本报告翻译了英文提要。

上述各方面的专家和技术人员，在此一并表示衷心感谢！发掘过程中，良渚遗址管委会也给予了大力支持，特别是张炳火主任曾想把卞家山发掘区作现场保护，虽然最后没有成功，但他为此付出的努力我们都铭记在心。

赵晔

2013年12月

# REPORTS OF THE GROUP SITES AT LIANGZHU, VOLUME VI

# Bian Jia Shan

(Abagraph)

Bian Jia Shan site is located at Pinyao town of Yuhang district, Hangzhou city of Zhejiang province, which is in the south of Liangzhu site complex area, about 1 kilometer south of Mo Jiao Shan central settlement. The main part of Bian Jia Shan site is a long strip mound that runs from east to west, about 800 meters in length and 30 - 50 meters in width, 1 - 2 meters above the modern paddies. It was called "Yu Tian Li" (Jade fields) on account of jades unearthed here in the Republic of China period (1912 - 1949). It was confirmed as a Liangzhu culture site by field survey in 1999. In 2002, wooden post remains were found by a pilot excavation in the west of the site. Afterwards, there were three seasons of archaeological excavations conducted from 2003 - 2005, opening in total an area of 2600 square meters. The important discoveries include a Liangzhu culture cemetery, drainage ditches, a wooden pier, etc. In total, 66 burials, 1 house remain, 5 pits and 3 ditches were excavated, as well as more than 1400 artifacts including pottery, stoneware, jade, bone - tooth ware, lacquer ware and bamboo - weaving remains.

The archaeological work reveals that the north part of excavation area is a cemetery from early – mid to late Liangzhu periods; while in the middle part of the site two phases of drainage ditches were successively used in the mid and late Liangzhu periods. In the south part of the site was a late Liangzhu water port and pier. The site expanded from north to south, over a duration of a few hundred of years.

The north cemetery can be divided into four stages. The average size of burial pits is about 1.8 - 2.75 meters long, 0.5 - 0.95 meters wide, and 0.1 - 0.84 meters in depth from the current top surface. A few burials had the preserved wooden structure of coffin remains, which indicate that the shape of the coffin had curved base and top, consisting of two curved pieces. Very few burials had the remains of skeleton proving limited data on body position. The orientation of burials are half northwards and half southwards, with very few towards to the east; this is quite different from the south - oriented burial tradition of most Liangzhu graves. More than 460 burial objects were found from those burials, consisting of about half pottery, a number of jades and a few stone wares. The fundamental assemblage of pottery is sets of a ding tripod, dou stemmed plate, guan pot and pen basin. Pottery spinning

wheels, cups and drinking pots are also common types. Jade types included the comb - head, bracelet, huang semi - circle pendant, awl - shaped object, beads and tubes. The yue perforated axe is the main type of stone object, while stone adze, knife or arrow - head were occasionally buried. In addition, the lacquer drinking vessel gu can be seen in a couple of cases in terms of impressed marks in the soil.

The two successive drainage ditches were found stratigraphically at the same place in the middle area. The ditches were close to the east end, and run from east to west then turning to the south into the naturally watered land in south. G1 is more than 10 meters wide, with a bamboo fence and wooden posts along its banks. G2 is underneath G1, is bigger, and had better preserved bamboo fencing and wooden posts. The north bank of both ditches had a port laid with stone foundations, and built up with wooden posts. The east end of ditches had fill deposits with a large amount of aquatic species remains, such as snail shells, razor clam shells and clam shells, which probably to came from the settlement area towards further east, as suggested by the coring survey.

The south port and pier were built on a man - made earthen platform. 150 wooden posts were found in a layout that was square angled in shape. The posts along the bank were in three parallel rows running from east to west, which were the pile foundations for the port. Protruding posts of another side, at a right angle, running towards the water were bigger and clustered, and were the the pile foundation for a pier.

Most artifacts were found from the deposited layers in the ditches and near the port. These included more than ten thousand pottery sherds, and almost one thousand pieces of various other types of artifacts. Additionally, there are a number of animal bones such as pigs, water buffalos and deer, as well as plant remains of charred rice (Oryza sativa), south sour jubjube (Choerospondias axillares), water caltrop (Trapasp.), foxnut (Euryale ferox), melon (Cucumis melo) and bottle gourd (Lagenaria sciceraria). The miniature ceramic house, a square - shaped basin with four legs, a dustpan with a ring - shaped handle, and skull - shapedcontainer were all discovered the first time in Liangzhu culture. Many pottery were carved with refined decorative patterns, among which the brick - pattern is found first time. There are more than 100 incised and impressed symbols founded on the pottery. The unearthed lacquer wares were more than 70 pieces, including gu, dou, pan basin, tube shape container, spoon, paddle, chui hammer, sandal, whipping top, handle, lid, ball, construction part and so on. The gu drinking vessel has the similar shape and style as Shang - Zhous bronze gu, and some of them also have a band of curved cloud pattern decorating the middle, like those found in bronzes. Lacquer gu is for the first time confirmed by preservation of real objects, which explains the similar lacquer marks found from other Liangzhu sites previously.

Bian Jia Shan site, with its abundant, unique features and artifacts, demonstrates many details of

ancient daily life in the Liangzhu period. The discovery of a port and pier fills a gap in our knowledge of settlements in a wetland. The miniature house provides important evidence for the reconstruction of Liangzhu buildings. A number of carved symbols contribute to the dataset for understanding the origins of the Chinese writing system. And, the number and quality of lacquer wares are so far the best assemblage of these, which are rarely seen in prehistoric sites. In short, Bian Jia Shan will be another significant site in the study of Liangzhu settlement, subsistence and landscape, as well as for understanding the development and decline of Liangzhu society.
